TG Therapeutics (TGTX) Q3 2026 Earnings Call Transcript

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Hospital uptake peaked as a subcutaneous version moves toward quarterly dosing.

Need a quote from a Motley Fool analyst? Email [email protected]

TG Therapeutics, Inc. (TGTX +5.94%) reported a quarter of growth across its commercial and clinical operations, highlighted by the raising of its full-year global revenue guidance to 1 billion. Management stated that the company generated 85.9 million in operating income, supported by an expansion in the use of BRIUMVI among treatment-naive patients and younger demographics. The company focused on its capital allocation strategy by repurchasing 100 million of its common stock while maintaining a cash position of 556.7 million. Strategic development remains centered on life cycle innovations for BRIUMVI, including a simplified intravenous initiation dose and a subcutaneous formulation, alongside the advancement of its allogeneic CAR T cell therapy for progressive multiple sclerosis.

Operator: Greetings, and welcome to the TG Therapeutics Third Quarter 2026 Conference Call. [Operator Instructions] Please note, this conference is being recorded. I would now like to turn the call over to your host, Jenna Bosco, Chief Communications Officer. Please go ahead.

Jenna Bosco: Thank you. Welcome, everyone, and thank you for joining us this morning. I'm Jenna Bosco, and with me to discuss TG Therapeutics' Third Quarter 2026 financial results are Michael Weiss, our Chairman and Chief Executive Officer; Hari Miskin, our Chief Development Officer; Adam Waldman, our Chief Commercial Officer; and Sean Power, our Chief Financial Officer. Following our safe harbor statement, Mike will begin with an overview of our recent corporate developments. Hari will then discuss highlights from our recent data presentation, followed by Adam, who will provide an update on our commercial efforts. Sean will conclude our prepared remarks with a review of our financial results before we open the call for Q&A.

Before we begin, I'd like to remind everyone that today's discussion will include forward-looking statements within the meaning of the Private Securities Litigation Reform Act of 1995. These statements may include expectations regarding our future operating and financial performance, including sales trends, revenue guidance, projected milestones, development plans and the outlook for our marketed products and our pipeline products. Please note that these statements are subject to risks and uncertainties that can cause our actual results to differ materially from those indicated. These risks are detailed in our SEC filings. Additionally, any forward-looking statements made today reflect our views only as of this date, and we disclaim any obligation to update or revise them.

As a reminder, this conference call is being recorded and will be available for replay for the next 30 days on our website at www.tgtherapeutics.com. With that, I'll now turn the call over to Mike Weiss, our CEO.

Michael Weiss: Thank you, Jenna, and good morning, everyone. We appreciate you joining us today. I'm pleased to report another strong quarter for TG with meaningful progress across both our commercial business and development programs. Global revenue for the quarter was approximately $260 million, including $233 million in U.S. BRIUMVI net revenue. With solid third quarter performance and trends pointing to a strong fourth quarter, we're raising our full year global revenue guidance to $1 billion for 2026, a pretty remarkable accomplishment less than four years after launching BRIUMVI. I'd like to pause here to congratulate our team for their hard work and dedication to the MS community and to TG.

And as they know, we're just getting started. $1 billion in annual revenue is an important milestone, but I don't believe it comes close to defining the opportunity ahead of us. On our last call, I described BRIUMVI as a starting line for TG, providing the commercial capabilities, financial resources and scientific foundation to build a value creation engine. And I think the progress we've made over the last several months offers a compelling example of what that means. And nowhere was that progress more evident than at this year's ECTRIMS meeting in Toronto.

We presented an extensive collection of data ranging from seven-year clinical outcomes to new real-world evidence, life cycle innovations and the first clinical results from azer-cel and progressive MS. Given the breadth of these presentations, I thought it would be valuable for investors to hear directly from our Chief Development Officer, Hari Miskin, who will take you through the key findings shortly. But first, I wanted to share some of my own observations from the conference. I had the opportunity to meet with over 100 physicians and advanced practice providers during the conference. Conversation after conversation reinforced just how far we've come and the growing appreciation for the value BRIUMVI and TG are bringing to the MS community.

I was particularly encouraged by the growing recognition that not all anti-CD20 therapies are the same and that those differences may translate into meaningful differences in the treatment experience. What I found most interesting is how the data from ENHANCE and ENABLE are reinforcing some of these differences observed in clinical practice. And when clinical evidence meets personal experience, adoption follows. For me, that's how we'll achieve our ambition of becoming the most prescribed anti-CD20 therapy among new MS treatment starts. We believe two important innovations can help accelerate our progress toward that goal, the consolidated IV initiation dose and self-administered subcu BRIUMVI.

The first, which was studied in our positive Phase III ENHANCE study, would simplify the initiation of IV BRIUMVI by combining the day 1 and day 15 initiation doses into a single infusion. If approved, BRIUMVI will be the only IV anti-CD20 that offers the simplicity of a single initiation infusion followed every six months by a one-hour maintenance infusion. We believe that combination could represent a meaningful competitive advantage for IV BRIUMVI. In terms of timing, we're still targeting a potential approval of this new initiation dosing around mid-2027. The other subcu BRIUMVI represents our biggest near-term growth opportunity.

Today, approximately 35% of new anti-CD20 treatment starts are for patients choosing self-administration, a market in which BRIUMVI doesn't currently participate. And we believe this segment alone represents a multibillion-dollar opportunity. But here is what makes the opportunity particularly attractive to TG. We're not starting from scratch. We already have an established brand, a growing physician network and a commercial infrastructure serving the same MS community. And with the potential for quarterly self-administration, we believe subcu BRIUMVI could offer a compelling alternative to existing treatment options that require more frequent dosing. With Phase III results expected around year-end or early 2027, we're working toward a potential launch in 2028.

And strategically, we see subcu as additive to our IV business, not a replacement for it. Our goal is to make BRIUMVI a compelling treatment option regardless of how patients prefer to receive therapy because we believe no patient should have to choose their treatment solely based on route of administration. Beyond these life cycle innovations, TRS also offered an early glimpse of what could become an entirely new frontier in MS treatment, CAR T cell therapy. We are pleased to present the first clinical results from azer-cel, our allogeneic CAR T in progressive MS, where there remains a significant unmet medical need. Let me close with where I see TG today and where we are going.

We're delivering on our commercial commitments, raising our outlook and building a business that we believe has the potential to become substantially larger than it is today. And importantly, we don't believe our opportunities end with BRIUMVI in relapsing MS. We're exploring entirely new treatment approaches, including azer-cel and progressive MS, while advancing BRIUMVI in myasthenia gravis, treatment-resistant schizophrenia and potentially other indications. Each of these programs represents an opportunity to create additional value beyond our existing business. and we're pursuing them from a position of increasing financial strength. At the same time, we're committed to being disciplined with the capital we're generating.

Our objective remains clear: to build a company capable of repeatedly creating value through scientific innovation, disciplined execution and thoughtful investments that generate attractive long-term returns. I'm proud of what we've accomplished. But when I consider the opportunities in front of us, I believe we're still early in the stages of what TG can become. With that, I'll turn the call over to our Chief Development Officer, Hari Miskin, for a closer look at the clinical results from ECTRIMS and an overview of our development programs. Hari, welcome to the call.

Unknown Executive: Thank you, Mike, and good morning, everyone. I'm pleased to join you all today to provide an update on our clinical development programs and highlights of our recently presented data. As Mike mentioned, ECTRIMS was a particularly productive meeting for TG, the largest in the company's history in terms of the breadth and depth of data shared with presentations highlighting long-term outcomes of BRIUMVI, continued differentiation versus others in the class, progress across our life cycle programs and encouraging initial results from azer-cel in progressive MS. I'll begin with BRIUMVI, where investigators at ECTRIMS presented seven-year follow-up results from the ULTIMATE clinical development program. These results continue to demonstrate sustained efficacy.

Among patients who completed seven years of continuous BRIUMVI treatment, the annualized relapse rate was 0.018, roughly one relapse in over 50 patient years and approximately 88% remained free of 24-week confirmed display progression. To our knowledge, this is an unprecedented level of freedom from disability progression in longer-term follow-up studies originating from Phase III relapsing MS trials. The safety findings were also reassuring with no new safety signals observed over seven years of treatment. Together, these results reinforce our confidence in BRIUMVI as a long-term treatment option for people living with relapsing MS. We also presented updated real-world findings from ENABLE, our ongoing U.S. Phase IV observational study, which now includes more than 1,000 patients.

The results continue to reinforce the clinical profile we saw in our registration trials. 99% of patients remained relapse-free. Infusion times were consistent with our approved one-hour maintenance infusion and rates of infusion-related reactions were lower than those observed in the registrational trials. Turning to ENHANCE. This Phase IIb study initially focused on evaluating switches to BRIUMVI from prior anti-CD20 therapy and has evolved over time to also explore alternate dosing regimens for BRIUMVI, yielding a number of valuable data sets. The study culminated in a randomized trial to evaluate consolidating our approved two-dose initiation regimen into a single 600-milligram initiation infusion with the goal of making treatment initiation more convenient for patients and providers.

The trial met its primary endpoint, demonstrating bioequivalence between the two regimens. Importantly, day one infusion-related reactions were numerically lower with the single-dose initiation regimen, 16% compared to approximately 26% for the standard regimen with no grade 3 or higher infusion-related reactions in either arm. B-cell depletion and MRI outcomes were also comparable for both regimens. Together, these findings support the potential to simplify BRIUMVI initiation without compromising drug exposure or the observed safety profile. We are advancing the regulatory process and subject to approval, anticipate a potential update to our label to incorporate this new regimen around the middle of 2027.

We also presented an update on an earlier cohort from the ENHANCE study, which enrolled over 100 patients who transitioned directly from ocrelizumab to BRIUMVI. Approximately 60% of these patients reported experiencing wearing off symptoms while on ocrelizumab. However, following the transition to BRIUMVI, nearly 70% did not report this wearing off effect through week 48 of treatment. Interestingly, this switch cohort also contained a number of patients who experienced incomplete B-cell depletion or early reactants on ocrelizumab, yet we were able to obtain robust B-cell depletion with BRIUMVI. We believe these findings further support the potential for meaningful biological differences among anti-CD20 therapies, and we intend to continue investigating these observations and their potential clinical significance.

On that note, it's worth noting a recent publication in the journal Multiple Sclerosis and Related Disorders, which was a five-year comparative analysis of ublituximab against both ocrelizumab and ofatumumab. This peer-reviewed publication used an unanchored match-adjusted methodology to account for differences between the clinical trial populations to provide a statistically valid comparison. The findings were particularly interesting. Ublituximab was associated with a 44% lower estimated risk of confirmed 24-week disability progression compared with ocrelizumab and a 49% lower risk compared with ofatumumab. While these findings come from an indirect comparison rather than a randomized head-to-head trial, we believe the consistency of the results against both leading anti-CD20 therapies is thought-provoking and warrants further investigation. Now turning to subcu BRIUMVI.

The Phase I results presented at ECTRIM support our quarterly dosing strategy. Estimated subcutaneous bioavailability was approximately 62% and PK modeling of our 400-milligram quarterly dosing regimen predicts a geometric mean ratio of exposure relative to IV BRIUMVI of 1.20 with a lower bound of the 90% confidence interval that exceeds the non-inferiority threshold for success defined in our ongoing Phase III study. The pharmacodynamic findings were equally encouraging. All patients receiving the fully subcutaneous 400-milligram regimen achieved B-cell depletion within four days with depletion maintained throughout the dosing intervals. Importantly, the 400-milligram subcu injections were well tolerated with limited local and systemic injection-related reactions observed, all of which were grade one.

The Phase III study of subcu BRIUMVI is fully enrolled with top line results expected around year-end or early 2027. Assuming successful development and regulatory review, we continue to target a potential commercial launch in 2028. Lastly, turning to azer-cel, our allogeneic CD19-directed CAR T cell therapy, we presented the first clinical results from our Phase I progressive MS study in a late-breaking oral presentation at ATRS. The initial data set included 26 patients with progressive MS, all of whom had experienced disability progression despite high efficacy treatment, including prior anti-CD20 therapy. The median baseline EDSS score was 6.0, reflecting a population with substantial accumulated disability.

The early safety findings were encouraging with no dose-limiting toxicities observed across all dose levels, no Grade 3 or higher CRS, no grade 2 or higher ICANS, no GvHD and no grade 3 or higher infections observed. From a pharmacodynamic perspective, 25 of 26 patients achieved peripheral B-cell depletion, and there was early evidence of reduced B cell counts in cerebrospinal fluid, a compartment generally inaccessible to current anti-CD20 therapy. Turning to the preliminary clinical findings. At day 90 post CAR-T administration, nearly 90% of evaluable patients had stable or improved EDSS scores with the longest follow-up patients sustaining a two-point reduction for 1.5 years in counting.

While early, these observations are encouraging, particularly in a population with documented disability progression despite previous high efficacy therapy. We continue to follow these patients to assess longer-term outcomes while advancing dose optimization and look forward to sharing additional updates as the data mature. Finally, we continue to advance BRIUMVI into additional therapeutic areas beyond MS. Our Phase II study in generalized myasthenia gravis is evaluating whether rapid disease control with FcRn inhibition followed by BRIUMVI maintenance can provide more durable benefit while reducing the burden of ongoing treatment. This study has generated a lot of excitement as it explores a novel way to sequence therapies in MG to maximize their benefit.

We are also conducting an exploratory Phase II study in treatment-resistant schizophrenia to investigate whether B-cell depletion can provide clinical benefit in a patient population where emerging evidence suggests immune dysfunction may contribute to disease biology. Both programs are designed to generate meaningful clinical evidence with a disciplined approach to development investment and additional programs in other therapeutic areas are also under development. Looking ahead, the next several years will be an important period across our development programs, and our team remains focused on executing against these opportunities. With that, I'll turn the call over to our Chief Commercial Officer, Adam Waldman, for a commercial update.

Adam Waldman: Thank you, Hari, and good morning, everyone. We continue to build on BRIUMVI's commercial success with broader physician adoption and a growing base of patients on treatment. As Mike noted, third quarter U.S. BRIUMVI net revenue reached approximately $233 million, our 14th consecutive quarter of sequential growth. And as we discussed on our last call, we expected second half growth to be primarily driven by the fourth quarter, which is exactly what we're seeing.

With our core business metrics continuing to strengthen and a projected strong fourth quarter, we are raising our BRIUMVI U.S. net product revenue guidance from $890 million to $905 million to a new range of $915 million to $920 million, reflecting more than $260 million for the fourth quarter which takes the U.S. BRIUMVI business above $1 billion annualized revenue run rate for the first time. Behind these results, we continue to see progress across our key commercial priorities. BRIUMVI is strengthening its competitive position and has become an increasingly important choice in the MS market. As Mike and Hari discussed, our long-term and real-world data are helping physicians better understand BRIUMVI's differentiated profile.

These findings reinforce what physicians are seeing in practice and give them more reasons to consider BRIUMVI for their patients. We believe that combination of evidence and experience is building confidence and contributing to broader adoption. New patient starts continue to grow year-over-year, and we have seen an acceleration at the end of Q3 and continuing into Q4. Persistence remains strong. We're seeing a steady flow of new prescribers along with continued growth in repeat prescribing with October being a record for total BRIUMVI prescribers in a month. Our hospital business also delivered encouraging growth during the quarter. September and October were our strongest hospital months since launch.

We've consistently viewed the hospitals as an important opportunity, and we're beginning to see the benefits of the sustained effort we've made in this segment. Together, these trends are expanding our installed base of patients returning approximately every six months for treatment. And as that installed base grows, it creates an increasingly durable and predictable foundation of recurring revenue. We're also seeing an important evolution in where BRIUMVI fits in the treatment journey. Treatment-naive patients now represent approximately 40% of our new patient business compared with approximately 25% at launch. We're also seeing increasing adoption among younger women ages 18 to 40. This is an important shift.

It tells us that physicians are increasingly comfortable choosing BRIUMVI as an initial high-efficacy treatment rather than waiting until a patient has experience with another therapy. At the same time, approximately 1/3 of our new patient starts continue to come from patients transitioning from another CD20 therapy, a portion that has remained remarkably stable since launch. What's particularly encouraging is that we continue to attract these patients even as the competitive landscape evolves, including efforts by other manufacturers to transition patients from their IV to subcutaneous formulation. Taken together, these trends demonstrate the increasing relevance of BRIUMVI, both as an initial treatment choice and as an option for patients considering a change in therapy.

We're also beginning to see the benefits of the commercial investments we've made. Our expanded field organization is contributing to growth in new patient starts, by increasing educational efforts with established prescribers and expanding our reach among physicians with less BRIUMVI experience. Complementing that field effort, our direct-to-consumer initiatives are building awareness and confidence in BRIUMVI and encouraging conversations with health care providers. We're now seeing measurable results, including increases in aided and unaided awareness and prescription lift associated with our campaigns. We're also investing in data analytics and AI-enabled tools to improve field targeting, personalized physician education and help us deploy our commercial resources more effectively.

Our objective is straightforward: to support continued growth while increasing the productivity and scalability of our commercial organization. Looking ahead, we see two important opportunities to significantly expand the BRIUMVI franchise. The first is the simplified IV single-dose initiation regimen. We see this as more than an incremental enhancement. Based on physician feedback and our market research, we believe eliminating the second initiation infusion could become a meaningful driver of additional demand, providing further potential tailwinds to our IV business in the second half of '27 and beyond. With that in mind, we're actively preparing for a potential launch around the middle of 2027. The second opportunity is our investigational subcu BRIUMVI formulation designed for self-administration once every three months.

We believe the addition of subcu can more than double BRIUMVI peak sales opportunity by opening an entirely new segment of the anti-CD20 market where we don't currently participate. And importantly, we believe we're particularly well positioned to capture this opportunity with an established brand, broad physician awareness and existing commercial infrastructure, we have the potential to generate substantial new revenue without having to build an entirely new commercial organization. That's the operating leverage that makes this opportunity especially attractive. As Mike discussed, our strategy is to continue supporting both IV and subcu BRIUMVI. We're not looking to replace one business with another. We're looking to build 2 substantial businesses under one brand.

And with our intellectual property portfolio extending into the mid-2040s, we believe we have the opportunity to continue investing in both formulations for years to come. Together, the opportunity to reach more patients, build on our existing commercial infrastructure and invest behind a long patent runway gives us confidence in BRIUMVI's substantial long-term growth potential. In summary, we continue to build momentum. Our core business metrics support our increased full year U.S. revenue guidance of $915 million to $920 million and our expectation for a strong fourth quarter and full year 2026. With future opportunities to strengthen IV and a potential multibillion-dollar opportunity in self-administered treatment, we see substantial growth ahead.

Our focus is on finishing 2026 strong and capitalizing on the opportunities in '27 and beyond. With that, I'll turn the call over to Sean for a review of our financial results.

Sean Power: Thanks, Adam. Let me walk through the financial results for the quarter and touch on a few items worth highlighting. As Mike and Adam mentioned, commercial performance remains strong. U.S. BRIUMVI net product revenue was $233 million in the third quarter, up 52% from a year ago. Total net product revenue was $240 million when including $7.4 million in product sales to Neuraxpharm, our ex-U.S. partner. We also recognized $19.9 million of license, milestone, royalty, and other revenue, including a $10 million sales milestone from our ex-U.S. partner, bringing total revenue for the quarter to approximately $260 million, up 61% year-over-year. U.S. BRIUMVI gross margin remains strong and consistent at 87% for the quarter.

Consolidated gross margin was approximately 84%, reflecting the mix of U.S. sales, partner shipments, and other revenue. Turning to expenses, R&D and SG&A, excluding stock-based compensation, totaled approximately $107 million for the quarter, including roughly $10 million related to subcutaneous BRIUMVI manufacturing and secondary source manufacturing. This compares to $150 million in total operating expenses in Q2 of this year, with the sequential decline driven primarily by lower spending on those manufacturing activities, which were particularly elevated in the prior quarter. With that spending profile, we generated operating income of $86 million for the quarter, compared to $29 million in Q3 of last year. Net income was $55.5 million, or $0.35 per diluted share.

One point worth noting on the year-over-year comparison, Q3 of 2025 included a substantial non-cash income tax benefit from the release of our deferred tax valuation allowance, which significantly increased reported earnings in that period. Turning to the balance sheet and capital allocation. We ended September with approximately $557 million in cash equivalents, and investment securities. During the third quarter, we repurchased $100 million of our common stock, representing roughly 2 million shares at an average price of approximately $49 per share. Across all of our repurchase programs to date, we've repurchased $300 million of our common stock, or roughly 8.9 million shares, at an average price less than $34 per share.

That represents nearly 6% of our shares outstanding when we initiated the repurchase program. We remain focused on disciplined capital allocation, balancing investment in the business with opportunities to return capital to shareholders. Looking ahead, we're raising our full-year revenue outlook to $915 million to $920 million in U.S. BRIUMVI net product revenue and approximately $1 billion in total global revenue, including projected payments from our ex-U.S. partner. On spending, we continue to expect full-year R&D and SG&A expenses, excluding stock-based comp, to be in line with our prior guidance of $450 million-$500 million, inclusive of those subcutaneous BRIUMVI manufacturing and secondary source manufacturer startup activities. Overall, it was another strong quarter financially.

We delivered significant revenue growth, generated meaningful profitability, and continued investing in the business while returning capital to shareholders. With that, I'll hand the call back over to the operator to begin the Q&A.

Operator: [Operator Instructions] And our first question will come from Michael DiFiore with Evercore...

Michael DiFiore: On the progress this quarter. Two questions for me. One on gross to net. I mean last month, one of your competitors noted that BRIUMVI was gaining share through aggressive contracting and that is probably not sustainable. And you've guided to gross to net averaging around 65% this year. So my question is whether you need a deeper gross to net in 2027 to maintain BRIUMVI's current payer access? And separately, my second question is, just given the BTK inhibitor data sets presented at ECTRIMS, I'm curious to get any updated thoughts on how we should think about this competitor class of drugs.

Michael Weiss: Great. Thanks, Michael, for the questions. Really appreciate it. Adam, maybe you could take part one, the gross to net in our competitors' comments. about our gross to net.

Adam Waldman: Yes. Thanks for the question, Mike. And first of all, I think it's great that the competitor is talking about us on their calls. Clearly, we're having an impact on their business. But to be clear, our contracting approach is both competitive and we believe 100% sustainable. Our strategy has always been to ensure we are competitive while maintaining discipline around the economics. And essentially, we want physicians to choose based on the clinical profile and the patients' needs. And we firmly believe physicians are reaching for BRIUMVI due to the clinical advantages of the drug.

As far as gross to net, your question there, I mean, over time, there will always be pressure on gross to net, not simply from contracting, but from payer mix, government discounts and other factors. We do use price increases to help offset the impact of these discounts to try to keep our net price as stable as possible. And our goal is to keep BRIUMVI competitive and accessible while building a profitable franchise. And you see here, we continue to raise our net revenue guidance, just raised it again today. So we believe our performance to date demonstrates we can do just that. So hopefully, that answers your question and puts that concern to rest.

Michael Weiss: Thanks, Adam. And as for the BTK question, so I'm going to start, and then I'll let Hari get a chance at his first Q&A on our quarterly call. But as I've said prior to any date on BTK, and my opinion hasn't changed that the BTK as a group, whatever comes of it, is really a competitor in the oral category. Even today, as we talk about 50%, 55%, whatever the exact number, it is somewhat hard to get exact on it that are going on to CD20s. That still means 50% to 45% are going on to something else. The vast majority of those are going on to the oral category, maybe upwards of 30%, 35%.

So it's a relatively large but genericized oral marketplace, which is where we think and have always believed the BTK class will fit in. So in terms of competitive dynamics, overall, we've never been concerned about it. We're still not concerned about it. But maybe, Hari, you can just give some maybe initial impressions on the actual data that we saw at the conference.

Unknown Executive: Yes. Thanks, Mike. I completely agree with you that this will probably complete primarily in the oral space. The class itself has unfortunately been plagued with issues and really not lived up to what we were hoping. I think remibrutinib has gotten closest with the cleanest safety profile. But unfortunately, the efficacy for that agent has really fallen short, I think, of what we were expecting in terms of relapse rate reductions and MRI outcomes, certainly when compared to CD20s.

It's also twice a day, which is worth noting because we continually hear that compliance is a key concern in this patient population, especially when you compare it to a twice a year IV option or hopefully a 4x a year subcu option. So we've had a number of discussions with clinicians and key opinion leaders, and we really don't see it competing in the high efficacy space with CD20s.

Operator: Our next question will come from Tara Bancroft with TD Cowen.

Tara Bancroft: This is Greg Torres on for Tara. Congrats on the progress. So in light of the guidance raise, how should we think about the exit velocity going into 2027, particularly with the simplified IV initiation potentially arriving around midyear?

Michael Weiss: Sure. Thanks for the question, Greg. Adam, do you want to chat a little bit about that?

Adam Waldman: Yes, sure. So as far as '27 goes, we've not given guidance for '27. We'll do that through the normal process, but I think it's worth reinforcing that our underlying business, the metrics continue to strengthen. New patient demand is increasing. What is Muse Ember? Meet Meta’s new low-cost AI model

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Booking a vacation sounds like one thing to hand off to an AI assistant, right up until it has to check the calendar, open a dozen tabs, and figure out whether the cheapest flight is still the cheapest once a carry-on is involved. Every one of those steps takes work, and when an AI agent is doing it, the cost can add up.

Meta has a new model built for that part of the job.

On Nov. 9, Meta's Superintelligence Labs introduced Muse Ember, which the company calls its smallest and most cost-efficient AI model yet. It's designed for the steps an agent might repeat dozens of times in a single assignment, like pulling details out of a document, calling another tool, or running a test after changing a line of code.

Muse is the app with the cute little avatar — named Jolly — that Meta has been promoting since September. The AI assistant can work across (some) apps with a user's permission. The app passed 5 million downloads in 22 days, and has already had many updates; last week, Meta started rolling out voice conversations and animated avatars for the assistant in the U.S. The best tech deal this week The Apple iPhone 18 Pro is free right now with T-Mobile Products available for purchase through affiliate links. If you buy something through links on our site, Mashable may earn an affiliate commission.

The Apple iPhone 18 Pro is free right now with T-Mobile

Meta also uses the Muse name for a family of AI models. The company positions Muse Wonder as the model for its toughest problems and Muse Spark as a model for coding and other work that involves using tools. Ember is the cheaper option for repeatable tasks. Meta's pitch is that developers can use Wonder to plan a complicated job and hand off the individual steps to Ember. For now, Meta lists Ember as available through its Muse Code tool and developer API.

In early testing shared by Meta, cloud storage company Box said it used Ember to check contracts clause by clause against a company’s negotiation rules and assign an overall risk rating. Meta says the model can take in up to a million tokens at once, enough to work through large sets of documents, and can handle images, audio, and video alongside text. That gives developers room to build an agent that can read a scanned contract or pull details from a meeting recording.

The price is what makes Ember interesting. On Artificial Analysis's benchmark, Ember and Anthropic's Claude Haiku 5.5 both rounded to a score of 36 at their highest reasoning settings. Ember cost about 14 cents per benchmark task, compared with 41 cents for Haiku, even though the two have the same standard rates of 10 cents per million input tokens and 50 cents per million output tokens. OpenAI's GPT-6 Luna scored 28 and cost about 15 cents per task at its highest setting.

In Meta's published results, Ember beat Haiku on a terminal coding test, while Haiku did better on a test that judged whether code changes were ready to merge. Meta added that it already has even cheaper models in training.

As the company continues to bet that AI assistants will take on more everyday tasks, developers will need an affordable way to handle all the little steps behind the scenes. A flight search, for example, can mean sorting through dozens of fares, each with its own rules. If Meta wants people to hand off that kind of chore every day, it has to make checking the carry-on policy cheap on its end, too.

Olivia Tauber is the deputy editor of digital culture, covering creators, media, movies, beauty, and more. Based in New York, her work has appeared in The New York Times, Vanity Fair, The Cut, Teen Vogue, Complex, and Interview Magazine. She holds a Master's degree in Journalism from NYU and a Bachelor's from the University of Michigan. She also runs Fan Mail, a weekly pop-culture newsletter.

Large-scale project renewals made a significant contribution to this quarter's growth. Beyond these renewals, customer demand remained robust across our digital solution portfolio. In our data center and cloud businesses, global RAM and memory supply constraints and geopolitical uncertainties have led to some delays in customer demand, creating a more challenging environment than initially anticipated. As a result, we expect a more gradual revenue growth trajectory this year. Our data center business continues to advance. We now have 54 megawatts of active IT capacity across 4 locations. Construction of the hyperscale data center facilities in Ankara for Google Cloud's Turkiye region is progressing at a rapid pace. I want to emphasize the strategic importance of data center investments.

With TRY 628 million invested to date in the data center and hyperscale infrastructure, we are building the foundation for our next phase of cloud and digital service growth, both for Turkiye and Turkcell. As we expand our digital capabilities, Techfin gives us another way to enhance customer engagement and diversify our revenues. Nongroup revenues accounted for 81% of the total, demonstrating Paycell's global market reach and its ability to scale well beyond the Turkcell ecosystem. Pay later transaction volumes grew 54%, while POS transaction volumes increased 94%, driving POS revenues to an all-time high. The POS business strengthens Paycell's position in the digital payments market. Paycell expands its reach, growing its active user base to 7.1 million users.

Financell also gained momentum with 3% quarter-on-quarter revenue growth in real terms. Looking ahead, the recent increase in the device price limit for 12-month installment plans from TRY 20,000 to TRY 40,000 creates further opportunities for growth in Q4. Kamil Kalyon: Thank you, Ali Taha Koc. Let me turn to our third quarter financial results. It was another excellent quarter for Turkcell. Building on our strong first half performance, we accelerated revenue growth and delivered further improvement in profitability. Our disciplined pricing strategy, together with robust momentum in our corporate business, was instrumental in delivering these results. This performance underscores the strength of our diversified business model and our continued focus on profitable growth. Group revenue reached TRY 82 billion, representing an impressive year-on-year increase of 6.2%. Turkcell Turkiye remains the primary engine of group growth, supported by accelerating activity in the corporate segment. Meanwhile, consumer revenues returned to positive growth, supported by our pricing actions and increasingly rational market dynamics.

This marks an important turning point, signaling positive shift in the segment's growth trajectory. Turning to profitability, I would like to highlight 2 key achievements. First, we expanded our EBITDA margin despite a higher revenue contribution from hardware sales in the corporate segment and POS services, both of which carry structurally lower margins. Second, and perhaps even more notably, this improvement came on top of an already exceptional EBITDA margin of 43.9% in the same quarter last year. As a result, we achieved an outstanding EBITDA margin of 44.2%, exceeding expectations and further demonstrating our ability to deliver profitable growth through disciplined execution. Next slide, please. Moving to net income.

Let me walk you through the main factors driving this quarter's results. The amortization of 5G license accounted for approximately half of the year-on-year increase in depreciation. At the same time, the capitalized license continued to generate significant monetary gains, providing a favorable offset. On the financing side, our net FX loss was driven by several factors, including outstanding 5G license installments, higher year-on-year TL depreciation, and a larger short FX position amid elevated hedging costs. To mitigate the impact of rising foreign exchange losses, we closely monitor market conditions and dynamically manage our cash position by taking proactive measures to capture favorable opportunities. In this context, our FX swap transactions have enabled us to generate substantial interest income.

Turning to our data center investment, we activated the last module of our data center in Ankara as outlined last quarter. Turning to our balance sheet. We closed the quarter with TRY 99 billion in cash, maintaining a strong liquidity position and ample financial flexibility to pursue our strategic priorities.

Nevertheless, our leverage improved quarter-on-quarter, with net debt-to-EBITDA declining 0.31x, remaining comfortably below peer levels. Now let's take a closer look at our FX exposure. Next slide please. Our treasury strategy continues to balance attractive returns with prudent balance sheet management. We use FX swaps selectively to benefit from favorable TL yields where market conditions support their use.

At quarter-end, 67% of our cash, excluding FX swaps, and 87% of our financial debt are -- were denominated in hard currencies. We also maintained substantial hard currency liquidity as a neutral hedge against our FX liabilities. Following the removal of tax advantages for money market funds and ongoing CapEx payments, we significantly scaled back our FX swap book activities starting in early Q4. Long-term hedging costs remain elevated at approximately 40% annually for instruments that do not introduce additional FX risk. With the Central Bank's managed exchange rate policy expected to remain broadly unchanged, we intend to maintain our net short FX position near its current level and continue to benefit from this approach.

At the end of the third quarter, our FX debt stood at USD 4.3 billion, compared with USD 2.4 billion in FX denominated financial assets. In addition, we had a derivative portfolio equivalent to USD 1.4 billion. Our net short FX position, therefore, stood at approximately USD 1.3 billion, comfortably within our medium-term target range of plus or minus USD 1.5 billion. Creality Debuts mBox, A Kid-Operated AI 3D Printer, at $329

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

HOUSTON, Nov. 9, 2026 /PRNewswire/ -- Creality, a global leader in the 3D printing industry, today introduces mBox, an AI 3D printer designed specifically for kids. Featuring a 153 Ã 153 Ã 153 mm build volume, the kid-operated printer brings together kid-safe designs, simple printing operations, and accessible creative tools to help young makers turn their ideas into physical creations. Priced at $329, mBox is now available exclusively on TikTok Shop in the U.S. from November 9 to December 10.

mBox is built for families with children aged 6â16 who enjoy drawing, block building, model making, DIY projects or exploring technologies. It is particularly designed for young creators who want to go beyond just buying ready-made toys and instead turn their own imagination into tangible and personalized creations, whether through independent projects at home or hands-on activities in the company of parents. mBox is also ideal for STEAM learning spaces where educators seek to integrate safe and simple 3D printers into everyday classroom subjects, like science experiments, engineering design, and creative arts.

Developed specially for kids, mBox prioritizes safety in its product engineering. Its fully enclosed chamber keeps the nozzle and moving components behind an enclosure while children watch their creations take shape. During printing, the unheated build plate eliminates the need to handle a heated bed when removing finished prints, while activated carbon filtration helps filter impurities from the chamber air.

Notably, a configurable door-open alert offers three response options: no action, alert only, or alert and pause. When the alert-and-pause is set and triggered, printing stops and parents receive an app notification.

Together, these thoughtful design features help create a safe and friendly space for printing and creating, ensuring parents feel more at ease as children build 3D models on their own.

mBox focuses on simplified operations to make 3D printing easier for kids to get started with.

With the 3.97-inch full-color touchscreen, kids can pick a model and start printing in just a few clicks without connecting to a phone. Before printing, auto leveling simplifies print preparation and helps improve first-layer adhesion, reducing the need for parents' manual adjustments. During printing, the CoreXY motion system with vibration compensation supports stable printing and cleaner surface details, while AI detection uses the built-in camera to identify issues like warping, foreign objects, and spaghetti failures, and then alerts are sent to parents through the app.

In addition, there is RGB status lighting to show print progress and a low-noise mode which keeps operating noise at or under 46 dB.

Beyond the printer itself, mBox bridges kids with dedicated software, Creality Park App and Creality Park Studio. The Creality Park App is a 3D playground showcasing curated models and STEAM courses in a fun way and offering pick & print experience for younger users. For those ready to take their ideas further, the Creality Park Studio, a PC-based platform, offers approachable 3D modeling tools to help design original creations.

The ecosystem also incorporates AI tools CubeMe and SnapForm, enabling kids to turn photos into personalized, printable figurines, keychains, and accessories.

From a simple idea to a piece of creation, mBox aims to spark imagination and encourage hands-on making, helping young creators turn inspiration into something they can hold in hands.

mBox is now available exclusively on the U.S. TikTok Shop at the price of $329 from November 9 to December 10.

QC Inc (QUBT) Q3 2026 Earnings Call Transcript

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Management details the shift from research toward scalable, commercial manufacturing.

Need a quote from a Motley Fool analyst? Email [email protected]

Quantum Computing Inc. (QUBT +8.37%) reported record quarterly revenue driven by the integration of acquired photonics and advanced packaging businesses. Management reported a strategic shift from research and development toward scalable manufacturing and commercial deployment, prioritizing the growth of its photonic and advanced packaging products. The company is focusing on integrating 11 facilities across eight states to support its quantum roadmap and improve operating efficiency. While the company maintains a substantial cash position of $1.3 billion, it remains focused on integrating recent acquisitions and scaling production to meet demand in aerospace, government, and industrial markets.

Operator: Good day, ladies and gentlemen. And welcome to the Quantum Computing Inc. Third Quarter 26 Shareholder Conference Call. At this time, all participants are in a listen-only mode. Following today's prepared remarks, the line will be open for questions. It is now my pleasure to introduce you to your host, David Moskowitz. Head of investor relations at QCI. David, please go ahead.

David Moskowitz: Thank you, operator, and welcome to QCI's third quarter 26 financial results and business update conference call. Before we begin, please note that today's remarks include forward looking statements including statements about our expected results, plans, strategy and market opportunities. These statements are based on current expectations and involve risks and uncertainties that could cause actual results to differ materially. These risks are described in today's press release and in our SEC filings including our most recent Forms 10-K and 10-Q. These statements speak only as of today, and we undertake no obligation to update them except as required by law. On the call today, we have Dr.

Yuping Huang, QCI's Chairman, Chief Executive Officer and Christopher Bruce Roberts, our CFO. The team will provide an update on the business followed by a question-and-answer session. I will now turn the call over to Yuping. Please go ahead.

Yuping Huang: Thank you, David. Good afternoon, everyone, and thank you for joining us. Looking at our third quarter and the year so far, I believe QCI is entering an important new stage. We have spent years building photonic based quantum technology designed for the real world. Now we are beginning to see our investments come together and our attention is shifting from what we can build to what we can put to work for customers. That means getting our technologies into customers' hands. Delivering on our commitments and building a business that can grow sustainably. Execution matters. More than ever. Our third quarter results reflect progress in that direction.

In Q3, we generated a record revenue of $9.5 million led primarily by our expanded photonics business which serves customers across a range of established markets. The acquisitions completed this year added established products and customer relationships. As well as manufacturing capabilities, essential to our quantum road map. That gives us commercial opportunities to grow revenue today while supporting the technologies we are developing for the future. During the quarter, we provided cloud access to our Directory system to Hamed Khalifa University in Qatar. Supporting the research education and adoption of quantum computing and networking.

That builds early progress including the installation of a directory optimization system at a leading global consulting firm and the agreement with Planck Dynamics for our neural wave reservoir computer in defense applications. More recently, we announced our next generation Dirac-3 quantum optimization machine which is designed to solve optimization problems with up to 9.98 thousand variables, an important step. In our quantum computing road map. We expect the first customer shipment during the fourth quarter. As a part of the announcement, we published the benchmarking results involving problems with up to 9.98 thousand variables, comparing the system's performance with leading classical optimization solvers.

We are encouraged by these results and what they tell us about the progress in developing increasingly capable quantum systems. This is still work to do as we evaluate performance across different applications. But advances like these bring us closer to solving larger more complex problems for customers. These are encouraging developments for sure. But what matters most is what customers can ultimately do with our quantum technology. Our recently announced collaboration with ImmuBio is a good example. Our collaboration with ImmuBio is exploring how direct optimization AI and the conventional analytics could potentially identify new insights for clinical research data. It is early work, but it reflects what matters most to me.

That the true measure of innovation is not just what a technology can do, but what it enables others to achieve. that is exactly the thinking behind the broader platform we are building. Our quantum photonics platform, extends beyond computing into sensing and communications. as well. Some applications are commercial today while others need further development and validation. Our manufacturing capabilities connect these opportunities. By combining photonic design, chip manufacturing, and advanced packaging at our North Carolina facility and our thin film lithium niobate facility in Arizona. We gain greater control over development and production. This infrastructure serves 2 important purposes. Supporting our proprietary quantum systems and serving established photonics markets.

The task now is to integrate it effectively and deliver for customers. With that in mind, we are focused on 3 priorities over the next, 12 to 24 months. The first is growing our commercial business. We have an opportunity to build on the established businesses and customer relationships we have added. While expanding the market for our quantum and advanced photonics systems. Our photonics and advanced packaging businesses provide a commercial foundation we can grow today. While supporting the development of our quantum technologies. As those technologies mature, we see opportunities to introduce them to a broader range of customers.

Our goal is to turn growing interest in QCI into orders. a stronger backlog, deployments, and the lasting customer relationships. Second is to scale manufacturing. We are bringing operations together expanding capacity and improving efficiency. Reliable repeatable production matters as our quantum technologies mature. Our third priority is advancing our quantum portfolio. We have achieved all the objective outlined in our 24 to 26 portfolio road map. So we are moving into the new phase to scale our manufacturing and product lines. Our goal is to make quantum systems smaller more capable, and easier to deploy. A major part of that work involves integrating more of our technology onto photonics chips.

Which brings us closer to the compact practical systems we envision for the future. These 3 priorities are closely connected. Our quantum road map guides the technologies we develop our manufacturing capabilities, help us bring those technologies into production. And our commercial efforts helped us understand where customers see value and where we should focus our resources. It is how we move from building the technology to build a sustainable business around it. That brings me back to something fundamental about QSAR and why we have chosen photonics as our approach. I believe for many years, that light offers 1 of the most promising path to making quantum technology practical and accessible. There are several approaches to quantum computing.

As we all know, each with its own strength and challenges. We choose photonics because we believe it offers important advantages in bringing quantum technology into broad real world use. Our direct systems, for example, operate at room temperature, with relatively low power requirements and without the specialized cryogenic infrastructure. Required by many other approaches. That can make a meaningful difference in terms of cost. And the complexity. Photonics also allows us to build on decades of advances in optical technology and the semiconductor manufacturing. And with the capabilities we have assembled at QSI, we are bringing more of that process under our own control. This is still considerable work ahead.

But I believe this approach gives us a strong foundation for the future. My ambition has always been to make quantum technology useful and accessible on the much broader scale. And I believe Light offers a practical near term path toward that goal. When you are taking the journey 1 step at a time. And I am encouraged by the progress we have made. With our solid Q3 results as evidence. With that, I will turn the call over Christopher to review our third quarter financial results and how we are managing our resources to support the work ahead. Christopher?

Christopher Bruce Roberts: You, Yuping, and good afternoon, everyone. I will now review our third quarter financial performance our balance sheet and how we are approaching capital allocation as we move into the next phase of QCI's development. Starting with revenue, we generated $9.5 million in the third quarter up from $384 thousand in the same period last year. To put that growth in perspective, our third quarter revenue exceeded the $9.2 million of revenue generated during the entire first half of 26. Revenue was primarily driven by sales of photonic products serving aerospace, government and industrial applications. The businesses we acquired earlier this year have expanded our commercial foundation and are contributing to our financial performance.

While also supporting our longer term quantum technology road map. We generated $428 thousand in gross profit during the quarter, for a gross margin of 4.5%. While margins remain modest, we are encouraged by the progress. As revenue grows, we see opportunities to improve manufacturing utilization increase operating efficiency, and expand margins over time. Turning now to expenses. Our operating expenses totaled $29 million during the quarter compared with $10.5 million in the same period last year. The increase is largely due to higher personnel and related costs associated with our expanded R&D sales, and marketing activity as well as approximately $1 million in acquisition related expenses.

Interest and other income totaled $13 million during the third quarter, compared with $3.5 million a year ago. Primarily reflecting interest earned on our larger cash and investment balances. For the third quarter, we reported a net loss of $15.1 million or $0.07 per basic share. Compared with net income of $2.4 million or $0.01 per basic share in the prior year period. The change from net income to net loss is largely due to higher operating expenses as we have expanded the business and smaller mark to market gains on a warrant derivative. Partially offset by increased revenue and interest income. Turning now to the balance sheet.

We ended the third quarter with approximately $1.3 billion in cash, cash equivalents, and investments. Consistent with our position at the end of Q2. Total assets were approximately $1.6 billion at quarter end while total liabilities were $47.7 million We also ended the quarter with approximately $39.1 million in contract backlog. Providing a foundation of business contracts as we expand customer relationships and convert opportunities into revenue. As of 9/30/2026, we had approximately $174.3 million in cash and cash equivalents. Approximately $1.1 billion in investments approximately $916.9 million in working capital.

As a result, we believe our liquidity is efficient to fund our operations for at least the next 12 months giving us flexibility to support both our current operations and our longer term technology development plans. At the same time, mindful of financial discipline as we move from building our capabilities to scaling the business. The acquisitions completed earlier this year were designed to strengthen our technology and manufacturing foundation while adding established commercial operations. Our focus now is on integrating those businesses effectively investing where we see opportunities for growth, and improving operating efficiency over time.

We will continue to evaluate opportunities including potential acquisitions, that can advance our strategy, but any future investment must support our priorities and make sound financial sense. We believe we have the resources and capabilities to execute on our plans and the responsibility to deploy our capital thoughtfully to drive sustainable growth and long term shareholder value. With that, I will turn the call back to Yuping.

Yuping Huang: Thank you, Christopher. Before we go to questions, I wanted to return to an important point. How we are moving from building our capabilities to putting them to work in the real world. I believe quantum technologies offers tremendous potential. But it is a yacht industry. And adoption will develop differently across markets and applications. Some customers need photonic technologies today while others are exploring what quantum systems can enable as they become more capable and practical. We need to understand these differences and be thoughtful about where we invest. As Chris outlined, our financial position gives us considerable flexibility particularly in the industry where technology takes time, to mature.

But having the resources to invest does not change our responsibility to use them wisely. We need to focus on where our technology can have the greatest impact. Where customers see value and where we can build a business that performs over time. I am proud of what our team has accomplished. We have brought together a remarkable group of people and their capabilities, and I believe we are in a much stronger position today than we were just 1 year ago. But there is a lot of work ahead. I have always believed that being early creates opportunity. But execution is what makes the opportunity last.

WEST LAFAYETTE — Purdue men's basketball is shaking up its starting lineup after an 0-2 start.

Harris is 3 of 6 this season, while the rest of the roster is 7-for-43.

Barium titanate's optical properties emerge more clearly after decades of widely varying measurements

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Other  |  Read Source →

A KAIST-led research team has characterized how light propagates through and is absorbed by barium titanate (BTO), a promising material for next-generation optical communication devices. By clarifying optical properties that had been difficult to determine because of the material's complex internal structure, the study provides a foundation for designing these devices.

Dinosaurs May Have Eaten Far More Fruit Than Scientists Ever Thought

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Science  |  Read Source →

An exceptionally preserved 74-million-year-old forest in New Mexico is revealing unexpected details about ancient plant life.

Dinosaurs may have been eating a much wider variety of fruits than scientists once believed. Fossils from a 74-million-year-old forest in New Mexico show that some flowering plants were already producing surprisingly large fruits millions of years before the asteroid impact that ended the age of non-avian dinosaurs.

The findings, published in the journal Science, challenge the longstanding theory that larger fruits and more complex seed dispersal strategies evolved primarily after the extinction event 66 million years ago. They also suggest that dinosaurs and other prehistoric animals may have helped spread the seeds of flowering plants much earlier than previously thought.

The research centers on an exceptionally preserved fossil forest near Truth or Consequences, New Mexico. James Saulsbury, a postdoctoral researcher at the University of Kansas Biodiversity Institute and Natural History Museum, helped collect, classify, and analyze the plant fossils discovered there.

“This paper focuses on a fossil forest in New Mexico and the preserved fruits, seeds and other reproductive structures of the plants that lived there,” Saulsbury said. “These fossils change how we understand the way flowering plants became modern in terms of their reproductive ecology.”

The forest was buried beneath volcanic ash that eventually hardened into rock. The preserved deposit stretches more than a kilometer (about 0.6 miles), capturing plants from approximately the same period and retaining evidence of how they were arranged across the ancient landscape.

“The New Mexico fossil site is exceptional,” Saulsbury said. “My co-authors have referred to it as a ‘botanical Pompeii’ because of its remarkable preservation. The site contains a layer of volcanic ash preserved as rock, known as tuff, that stretches for more than a kilometer. The deposits formed at essentially the same time, preserving many plants where they lived and died. It provides a snapshot of a forest from about 74 million years ago and even preserves aspects of its spatial arrangement.”

Flowering plants first appeared approximately 136 million years ago, but researchers have traditionally believed that their reproductive strategies remained relatively simple throughout much of the Cretaceous Period. Early flowering plants generally produced small seeds and fruits, with wind and other non-animal mechanisms thought to play major roles in seed dispersal.

“For much of their early history, they were relatively small, weedy plants that did not dominate forest canopies,” Saulsbury said. “Although they gradually diversified and occupied more ecological niches, it was thought their seeds and dispersal structures remained small and were primarily dispersed by wind or without fleshy fruit.”

Many early flowering plant seeds were thought to have ranged from dust-sized particles to roughly the size of poppy seeds. By contrast, the New Mexico fossils include fruits approximately as large as modern grapes. Such fruits are much larger than those previously expected from flowering plants of that age.

“The prevailing view has been that this more diverse reproductive ecology emerged only after the end-Cretaceous extinction,” Saulsbury said. “Our study suggests that transition began much earlier. The largest fruits in our fossil flora are about the size of a grape. That may not sound especially large today, but it is remarkably large for flowering plants of that period.”

The team examined hundreds of fossilized seeds, sorting them according to their physical characteristics. Because fossil material often lacks the features necessary to identify an exact species, researchers organized specimens into groups based on their shapes and structures.

“We sorted hundreds of fossil seeds into what we call morphotypes,” Saulsbury said. “These are groupings based on form rather than confirmed species because it is often impossible to determine whether similar-looking fossils represent one species or several.”

The fossil assemblage contains an unusual diversity of fruits, seeds, and reproductive structures. Although the exceptional preservation helped researchers recover this material, Saulsbury believes the forest itself differed from many other known Cretaceous environments.

“However, we don’t believe these findings are simply the result of preservation bias,” he said. “It appears to represent a genuinely unusual forest.”

Fruit size can provide information about the environments in which plants evolved and how their seeds may have spread. Today, many flowering plants produce fleshy fruits that attract animals, which can transport seeds away from the parent plant after eating the fruit.

“Large fruits tend to occur in moist environments with densely packed forests,” Saulsbury said. “Our findings indicate that modern-looking forest structure and reproductive ecology were developing earlier than previously believed. The diversification of flowering plant reproductive strategies did not have to wait until after the end-of-Cretaceous extinction.”

The discovery also complicates an established explanation for the evolution of fruit-bearing plants. Scientists had proposed that large, fleshy fruits became more widespread as mammals diversified following the extinction of non-avian dinosaurs, creating new opportunities for plants to disperse their seeds through animals.

“As mammals expanded into newly available ecological niches, flowering plants were thought to have diversified their seed dispersal strategies in response,” Saulsbury said. “Our findings suggest that story began earlier. If diverse, large fruits already existed in Cretaceous forests, they were likely being dispersed not only by early mammals but also by large dinosaurs and other animals.”

Reference: “Diversification of angiosperm reproductive strategies predated the end-Cretaceous extinction” by Jaemin Lee, Dori L. Contreras, James G. Saulsbury, Garland R. Upchurch and Cindy V. Looy, 25 June 2026, Science. DOI: 10.1126/science.adw9457

Rockets look to break two-game losing streak in winless white jerseys

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Other  |  Read Source →

If you believe in superstitions, the Houston Rockets have a perfectly good explanation for their first losing streak of the season.

Houston started the season hot, winning their first five straight games en route to an early 7-1 record before slumping to back-to-back losses in their last two contests against the Minnesota Timberwolves and the Phoenix Suns. While there are other reasons for the Rockets' two-game skid, namely their lack of center depth with backup big men Steven Adams and Clint Capela both missing extended time, some have pointed to Houston's newest jerseys as the reason.

The Rockets are 7-0 and undefeated in their red Icon and black Statement jerseys, but they sport an 0-3 record in their white Association jerseys, wearing them for their last two losses. While it's hard to blame the jerseys for Houston's losing streak, it has become one of the season's early talking points among Rockets fans.

With Houston again wearing its Association jerseys in a home matchup against the Denver Nuggets, the Rockets will look to break their two-game losing streak and earn their first win of the season in white.

Meta introduces Muse Ember to power cost-efficient AI agents

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Meta Platforms Inc. today introduced Muse Ember, an entry-level artificial intelligence model that costs significantly less than the competition.

Notably, it’s the first AI that the company trained using Muse Wonder. The latter algorithm is the Facebook parent’s flagship large language model. Meta didn’t specify which part of the workflow it automated with Muse Wonder.

One possibility is that the company used it to perform post-training, the task of honing a model’s capabilities by training it on additional data. Thinking Machines Labs Inc. recently demonstrated that even a relatively small LLM can run post-training runs end-to-end. Anthropic PBC, for its part, last week revealed that its AI models could soon gain the ability to significantly accelerate machine learning research.

Applications often use frontier LLMs such as Muse Wonder as part of AI ensembles. When a user enters a request, the frontier LLM splits it into smaller tasks and assigns them to agents powered by a lower-cost model. Meta says Muse Ember is specifically optimized to play the latter role.

The company evaluated the model using the Artificial Analysis Index, which aggregates nine AI benchmarks spanning use cases such as coding and financial analysis. Meta says that Muse Ember matched the score of Anthropic’s entry-level Claude Haiku 5.5 model at about a third the price. It also outperformed OpenAI Group PBC’s comparable GPT-6 Luna algorithm.

The same internal evaluation saw Meta test Muse Ember and the two rival models across nine other benchmarks. All three algorithms were in “max” mode, which means they used the maximum amount of hardware at their disposal instead of prioritizing cost-efficiency. Muse Ember outperformed Haiku and Luna across seven of the benchmarks.

The area where the model appears to have the biggest edge is business task automation. Whereas most of Muse Ember’s benchmark results were only a few percentage points better than those of Haiku and Luna, it clocked a 17% lead on AutomationBench-AA. It’s a collection of 657 tasks that span popular cloud applications such as Gmail and Salesforce.

Muse Ember has a context window of 1 million tokens, which enables it to ingest lengthy business documents. Users can also include images, audio and video in their prompts. The model generates responses up to 512,000 tokens in length.

Muse Ember is available through Meta’s Model API for 10 cents per million input tokens and 50 cents per million output tokens. A built-in cache can reuse frequently used prompts to avoid duplicate processing. When the feature is active, Muse Ember’s pricing drops to 0.4 cents per million input tokens.

Missouri basketball forward Mark Mitchell injury update

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Other  |  Read Source →

Fifth-year Missouri basketball forward Mark Mitchell will not return to the floor in the first half of the Tigers' game against Southern Illinois Edwardsville, a team spokesperson told the Columbia Daily Tribune, after he suffered what appeared to be an injury to his face in the opening minute of the contest.

The team spokesperson told the Tribune that Mitchell will be re-evaluated at halftime.

The star forward pulled in a rebound on MU's first defensive possession of the game, but collided with SIUE guard Dahrio McCaskill as he came back down to the court.

Mitchell, who was an All-SEC forward and led MU in scoring in each of the past two seasons, immediately fell to the court holding his face. Team trainers brought towels, and Mitchell appeared to be holding the towels to his nose.

He went to the locker room immediately with senior Mizzou athletic trainer Chris Perrin.

Perrin later returned from the locker room — without Mitchell — and delivered a message to Mizzou associate head coach Kyle Smithpeters. The team spokesperson confirmed shortly afterward that Mitchell will not return to the game in the first half.

More: Live updates as Missouri basketball takes on SIUE at Mizzou Arena

The Tribune will provide further updates on Mitchell's status if and when they become available.
